Table 6: Cells flagged by the held-out-tune coverage test of the 2D covariance, out of 300 pseudoexperiments per tune. A cell’s number is 16i + j for pT bin i and p∥ bin j, counted from zero.
Limits. The test resolves a per-cell deficit in content of about 0.10–0.13, and about 0.08
jointly; smaller deficits can pass. It is one-sided: over-coverage is reported, not failed. The pseudoexperiments depart from the null’s law in known ways: the flux and pair-band draws are not centred,
clipping truncates the shifts, and three terms of the covariance are not drawn (C08, Cmodel and the
simulation-statistics part of the statistical term), which can only lower the flag rate. G18_10a_02_11b shares its 2p2h model family with one of the model term’s generators. The run-period block was
not drawn.
Scope. The result concerns the procedure’s covariance at those two tunes, not the reported
intervals at the data, where the quoted covariance is made. It is not a coverage rate, says nothing
about truths unlike these two or about CPL or Cmodel , and gives no cause for the joint under-coverage
or for any flag. A held-out tune moves the truth, not the sample, so this is not the re-test on held-out
simulation of §5.2. It adds to the fixed-truth result of §5.2; it does not replace it.

5.4

Other validation diagnostics

Unbinned diagnostic (C2ST). In the classifier two-sample test (C2ST), a classifier is trained
to separate the data from the (reweighted) simulation in the full unbinned feature space, and its
held-out area under the ROC curve (AUC) or accuracy is compared with the chance value 0.5. The
AUC drops from ≈ 0.535 before unfolding to ≈ 0.501 after, indicating removal of the reconstructed
data ↔ MC mismatch. This binning-free check is descriptive, and no calibrated p-value is quoted.
As run, the C2ST trains and evaluates on the same sample without cross-fitting; a valid null would
require re-running the full cross-fitted OmniFold pipeline per pseudoexperiment. Calibrated unbinned
goodness-of-fit remains open [18].
Same-ensemble pull diagnostic (not a coverage test). An earlier ensemble of Ntoy = 200 2D
closure unfolds (Ref. [37], R1.2) uses weighted MC reco pseudo-data and stores the truth marginals
in truth histograms. Each toy has its own bootstrap seed k and per-event Poisson(1) draws, as in
production, with seeds chosen not to collide with the production replicas. Toy seeds are independent;
bins within a toy are correlated. The MC bootstrap multiplies the truth weight, so it fluctuates
both the unfolded result and the stored truth. The stored truth varies in all 205 reported bins, and
its all-toy mean X̄ truth is therefore not an independent fixed truth for a split-sample coverage claim;
it only centers a same-ensemble diagnostic.
In reported bin i with X̄itruth > 0, define σi as the across-toy sample standard deviation
(t)
(t)
(denominator Ntoy − 1) and standardized residual ri = (Ui − X̄itruth )/σi . Compare the per-bin
√
(t)
fraction ci of toys with |ri p
| ≤ 1 to the unit-normal Φ(1) − Φ(−1) = erf(1/ 2) ≈ 0.6827 and the
mean absolute pull ⟨|r|⟩ to 2/π. The fraction with |r| ≤p1 is 68.71 % (Gaussian reference 68.27%;
∆ = +0.44%; median 68.50%), and ⟨|r|⟩ = 0.794 against 2/π = 0.798 (within 0.5%). The signed

residual mean is +0.006 ± 0.082 σ, consistent with zero. The within-bin fraction is above 65 %
in 97.6 % of bins (only 5 of 205 fall below). These show that the standardized toy residuals are
approximately normal.
Because σi is estimated from the same toys that are evaluated, these figures diagnose standardized
pulls and Gaussianity only and are not a coverage test of the bootstrap band; no coverage number is
quoted from this ensemble, and the 2D coverage statements are the fixed-truth test of §5.2 and the
held-out-tune test of §5.3.
Stability and efficiency. With five and with ten iterations the total cross section agrees to within
0.026 %; the per-bin shape differs by 1.54 % RMS. The histogram-based and exact-split backends
agree on the total cross section to 0.04 %. Both comparisons were measured for the exact-split
cross-check. The selection efficiency (Fig. 6) reproduces Fig. 5 of the published measurement.

6

Results

The error bars on the unfolded points of Fig. 7 are the bin errors stored with the unfolded histogram
and its projections, not the covariance of §4. The published points in its top panel carry the square
root of the diagonal of the published statistical covariance, and Figs. 9–10 carry the same bin errors
as the unfolded points. The uncertainty budget of §4 is shown in Fig. 4 and Table 8.

6.1

Double-differential cross section

Figure 7 shows the unfolded d2 σ/(dpT dp∥ ) and its 1D projections. The total cross section is
3.069 × 10−38 cm2 /nucleon (published: 3.039 × 10−38 cm2 /nucleon).
The shapes are those expected for CC-inclusive scattering at ⟨Eν ⟩ ∼ 6 GeV. The pT projection
vanishes at pT = 0 and peaks broadly at pT ≈ 0.55–0.70 GeV/c, reflecting the Q2 ≲ 1 GeV2 scale of
few-GeV scattering through p2T ≃ Q2 Eµ /Eν , with Eµ /Eν ∼ 0.6 for these forward muons. It falls by

more than two orders of magnitude toward the 4.5 GeV/c edge. The p∥ projection rises from the
1.5 GeV/c threshold to a broad p∥ ≈ 3–4.5 GeV/c maximum (the NuMI medium-energy flux peak at
Eν ≈ 6 GeV), then falls by two orders of magnitude by p∥ ≈ 20 GeV/c. The θµ < 20◦ acceptance
empties the 2D map’s high-pT , low-p∥ corner (19 unreported diagonal cells; §3.5).

6.2

Comparison to the published measurement

The published and unbinned results share the data and the flux, detector and generator systematics,
and their cross-covariance is not available. Every χ2 and standardized difference here is therefore
descriptive, not a calibrated goodness-of-fit test.
Over the 205 reported bins, the total cross section reproduces the published result [3] to 1.0 %
(ratio 1.010; Table 7). The median per-bin ratio is 1.005; 86.8% of bins agree within 5 % and 95.1%
within 10 %. Indicative values of χ2 per reported bin are 2.27 with the published covariance alone
(analysed in §6.4) and 0.929 with the sum of the published covariance and that of this measurement.
The latter, χ2 /ndf = 0.929, double-counts the flux, GENIE and MINOS systematics that the
two results share. With the covariance of this measurement alone, χ2 is computed only in the
covariance’s well-constrained subspace, with a truncated spectral pseudo-inverse. The per-bin pulls
and the truncated result are therefore quoted together, and an unconstrained pseudo-inverse is not
used as a goodness-of-fit test on its own. The regularization scan (App. A.4) was made for the
exact-split cross-check on an earlier covariance and was not repeated for the quoted one.
Per-bin differences standardized by the published uncertainty (Fig. 8) are centered (mean 0.060),
with RMS 0.54, well inside the published uncertainties. Figures 9–10 compare the unfolded result
with simulated truth, the MC prior, in the published panel format.

6.3

Uncertainty budget

Table 8 gives the budget over the 205 reported bins, constructed as described in §4. Our own combined
budget, without the published covariance, is comparable to the published total: its per-bin median,
6.41 %, is smaller than the published 6.85 %, and its value on the integrated cross section, 4.76 %, is
larger than the published 4.61 % (the width-weighted total σ of App. A.3.2). The comparison leaves
out the generator-shape component Cmodel (§4.6), because the published uncertainty has no such
component either; with it, the per-bin median is 7.90 %. The systematic-universe block accounts
for a median 0.87 of the per-bin variance and 0.83 of the integral’s variance, and the run-period
block (§4.5) raises the per-bin median from 5.98 % to 6.41 %. The largest band is the flux, as in
the publication (Fig. 4), and it accounts for part of the difference between the per-bin medians: its
median is 3.71 %, against 4.01 % for the published flux block. The public release cannot establish
the origin of this difference, for which the FHC-only constraint file remains the leading, unconfirmed
hypothesis of this analysis. Our flux band carries the per-universe ν–e and inverse-muon-decay

constraint weights of the MINERvA Analysis
√ Toolkit. The statistical and ML blocks together
contribute under a tenth of the combined Tr C. Every block is computed with the LightGBM
estimator of the quoted central value (§3.2), and relative uncertainties are taken with respect to that
central value, which is the universe sweep’s central value at training seed 42. Taken with respect to
the exact-split cross-check instead, the same combined covariance has a per-bin median of 6.462 %.

matrix C; Tr C gives its absolute per-bin uncertainties in quadrature. The systematic row combines
43 of the 44 bands of the systematic-universe sweep (187 universes) with the normalization; the ML
row combines five training-seed settings, and the run-period row three run periods. Our combined
median relative uncertainty (6.414 %) is below the published total (6.85 %).

6.4

Eigenmode analysis of the published-covariance tension, χ2 = 2.27

With the published uncertainties as a descriptive scale, the central values have a pull RMS of 0.535
(a diagonal-only χ2 /ndf would be 0.29), whereas χ2 per bin with the full published covariance is 2.27.
The eightfold increase lies entirely in the off-diagonal structure of the published covariance, and
it is a difference in shape, not normalization (χ2norm = 0.04 against χ2shape = 465). The eigenmode
decomposition (Fig. 11) spreads it over many modes: the largest has a paper-covariance-standardized
component of magnitude 4.8, and the next nine 3.4–3.8. The 10 smallest-λ modes carry 9.6 % of the
total χ2 . Of the five largest contributors, two lie in the smallest-λ tenth of the spectrum. Restricted
to the 73 best-measured directions, χ2 = 136 (χ2 /205 = 0.66; the divisor is the number of reported
bins, not the number of directions kept). Of the six leading modes, the three in the highest-p∥ bins
are all poorly determined directions (λ/λmax ≲ 4 × 10−9 ); the other three are moderate-λ modes
(λ/λmax between 4 × 10−6 and 6 × 10−5 ) and lie below the highest p∥ bins (Fig. 12). No single pT or
p∥ band carries more than 15.2 % of the total.
With the purity-based subtraction, the choice of GBDT estimator spreads the distance over
about one unit, a methodological band: the exact-split cross-check gives 3.66, HistGBT 2.70 ± 0.04,
and LightGBM with seeds 1–10 gives 2.64–2.75. Beyond this estimator dependence, the source
of the shape difference has not been identified. A benchmark declared before it ran unfolds three
groups of run periods separately and compares combinations of them with the published result in
the directions of the published covariance. The run-period step does not account for the distance in
the best-determined directions, at the resolved size. In the other 132 poorly determined directions
of the published covariance, the combination of the other two run-period groups is farther from
the published result than the three-group combination, by 0.69 per direction, a fraction 0.31 of the
three-group distance, beyond noise estimated from 10 replicas. The noise is a lower bound, and
neither reading identifies a cause or which period, if any, is right. At the central value’s seed, the
refined negative-weight subtraction of the quoted result lowers the LightGBM χ2 per bin from 2.65
to 2.27. For the exact-split cross-check, χ2 is larger and distributed differently: its components of

again divided by the number of reported bins; App. A.5). Going from 5 to 10 iterations changes the
LightGBM (seed 1) value by a negligible +0.01. App. A.5 gives the full treatment and Figs. 11–12
the eigenmode-localization maps.

6.5

Comparison to GENIE MINERvA Tune v1

GENIE 2.12.6 [4] with MINERvA Tune v1 under-predicts the total cross section by ∼ 9 %
(σtune /σdata = 0.91), and the deficit is concentrated in the low-pT peak. Figure 13 compares
the prediction from the published ancillary files with both the published data and this measurement.
The data covariance strongly disfavors the tune, with χ2 /ndf = 33.0 for the published data and 31.3
for this measurement (26.5 for the exact-split cross-check), and our result tracks the data far more
closely than the model does (Fig. 14). The published analysis [3] also compares with NuWro [6],
GiBUU [7] and NEUT [43]. OmniFold’s per-event reweighted truth ensemble can be compared with
any such prediction in any projection of the classifier features without re-unfolding. An observable
outside that space requires a new unfolding, as in the 3D extension (§7), which adds Eavail as a
classifier feature.

7

Higher-dimensional extensions: available energy and momentum
transfer

Unbinned OmniFold adds an observable by adding an input to the step-1 and step-2 classifiers,
without an explicit migration matrix or per-bin response tensor. It avoids the combinatorial
binning penalty of matrix unfolding but still has a dimensional cost: classifier training, acceptance
and support coverage, sample sparsity and uncertainty quantification become more demanding as
observables are added. The primary methodological motivation is the absence of a practical analog of
D’Agostini iterative Bayesian unfolding (IBU). We extend the measurement to d3 σ/(dpT dp∥ dEavail )
with available energy as a third axis.
MINERvA has published binned triple-differential results (Fig. 15, Table 35), including recent
comparisons between low- and medium-energy beams [44]. The latter measure pT , p∥ , and available
recoil energy for a quasielastic-like signal, for which the available energy is the sum of proton
kinetic energies. This differs from the inclusive signal and species-dependent energy definition used
here. What distinguishes this analysis is not the number of dimensions by itself but the unbinned,
simultaneous CC-inclusive unfolding of three, four and five recorded high-level observables.
The comparisons with generator predictions in this section and in Sec. 8 are descriptive comparisons of central values and do not identify a nuclear or interaction mechanism (§7.7). In three and
four observables they use central values without uncertainties: no 3D or 4D covariance is quoted,
and no generator χ2 , p-value or significance (§§7.6 and 7.8).

cross-section measurements, by year, colored by channel (a double-differential d2 σ/dx dy counts

measurement; this work extends an unbinned CC-inclusive unfolding of high-level event features
to five recorded observables. Source: the MINERvA data-release page and the cited publications.
The plotted inventory runs through 2025; newer measurements are listed in App. E. Classification
uses each analysis’s headline differential result; flux, reconstruction, generator-tuning and total-only
entries are excluded.

7.1

Operational definition and provenance

Truth available energy is defined by its implementation, for which “low-recoil convention” is shorthand.
In MeV, the truth function (CVUniverse::GetEAvailableTrue()) computes

(E − 135) +
E+

π0

Protons and charged pions contribute the indicated kinetic-energy-like terms; neutral pions and
photons contribute total energy. All other final-state species contribute zero, including neutrons,
the primary muon, secondary e± , neutrinos, kaons, antiprotons, strange baryons, other mesons and
nuclear-remnant records. Reconstructed available energy is the MAT calorimetric quantity
h

) ,
(2)
implemented in the reconstruction function (CVUniverse::NewEavail()), with no final clamp to
non-negative values.
Both definitions are inherited from MAT-MINERvA rather than chosen for this analysis; the
comparisons below record differences between conventions, not the origin of the inherited one. There
is no unique published Eavail definition. Rodrigues et al. [5] give a closed list of five species: proton

and charged-pion kinetic energy plus π 0 , electron and photon total energy. Equation (1) includes
four of those five species and excludes secondary e± . The later definition of Ascencio et al. [8] is open:
it includes all other final-state particles except neutrons, subtracting a nucleon mass for strange
baryons, and so includes species omitted here. The charged-pion term is kinetic energy in Rodrigues
and in Henry/Su et al. Eq. 4 [45], and charged-pion total energy in the minerva-ml implementation
(App. F). We therefore do not claim that our definition matches “the published definition.”
The charged-pion term subtracts the inherited constant 135 MeV rather than the charged-pion
mass of about 139.57 MeV, and the production keeps that constant. Relative to the charged-pion
mass, it moves 0.65 % of the simulated signal weight into another of the seven true-Eavail bins of the
fine grid and changes no such bin by more than 0.97 %. This effect has not been assessed against
the projection of the five-dimensional covariance of the configuration-(a) central value. On the
sixteen-cell result of Sec. 10, from configuration (e), to first order in the unfolding and without
retraining, replacing the constant by the charged-pion mass moves the cross section by at most 0.19σ
in any cell and by 0.42σ as one coherent shift, the norm of the shift under the reported covariance
(0.21σ and 0.42σ without the run-period block).
The reconstructed coordinates share inputs. In units with c = 1, the implemented definitions
(with reconstructed muon quantities) are
Eνreco = Eµ + q0reco ,

Q2reco = max 0, 2Eνreco (Eµ − pµ cos θµ ) − m2µ ,
q
reco

= max{0, M 2 + 2M q0reco − Q2reco },

(3)

where m2µ = Eµ2 − p2µ and M is the average nucleon mass. The recoil-energy estimate q0reco differs
reco ; truth E
from the tracker-plus-ECAL available energy Eavail
avail also omits energy carried by species
such as neutrons and is not the full energy transfer. Thus five recorded coordinates do not establish
five independently constrained physical directions. Adding a coordinate, supplying a new detector
input, and resolving a new truth direction are separate questions.
Unfolding and binning. Both definitions are evaluated for all twelve ME FHC playlists, and, for
configuration (a), the 2D unfolding code is run with three classifier inputs, (pT , p∥ , Eavail ). Table 9
lists the configuration (§3.2) of each result. The cross section is extracted with the 3D bin volume
∆pT ∆p∥ ∆Eavail , and the flux Φ is still applied per pT bin, with the same value for every p∥ and
Eavail bin. The Eavail binning [0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 0.8, 1.5, 3.0, 100] GeV ends in a wide final bin, the catch
bin, so that the Eavail marginal contains the full CC-inclusive recoil tail. For configuration (a), the
overall completeness of the unfolding input (§3.4) is c = 1.0000, as in 2D.

7.2

The available-energy spectrum

The unfolded dσ/dEavail , the 1D projection of the 3D result (Fig. 16), falls monotonically from
2.18 × 10−38 cm2 /GeV/nucleon at the low-recoil peak to 6.7 × 10−41 in the catch bin, the expected
CC-inclusive shape with most of the rate at Eavail ≲ 0.8 GeV.

7.3

Validation against the 2D measurement

Marginalizing over Eavail should reproduce the 2D result of §6, our validation reference in the absence
of a directly matched published CC-inclusive 3D reference for this signal and phase space. The 3D
marginal (configuration (e)) and the 2D result ((f)) share the refined negative-weight subtraction but
differ in learner (Table 9). The normalization agrees closely: the marginal’s area-weighted total over
the 205 reported cells agrees with the 2D result’s to 0.141 %, and the median per-bin ratio is 1.0010.
The shape agrees less closely. The per-bin ratio of the marginal to the 2D result scatters by ∼ 3.7 %
(standard deviation over the 205 cells). With the published covariance, the indicative distance of
the marginal from the published result is χ2 /ndf = 4.52, above the 2D values: 2.27 for the quoted
LightGBM central value and 3.66 for the exact-split cross-check. Figure 17 (configuration (a)) gives
4.98. Because the two results share data and their cross-covariance is unavailable, this number is not
a calibrated goodness-of-fit statistic.

7.4

Closure: the one injected Eavail deformation is recovered

A reweighting closure tests the 3D unfolding of configuration (a) only, on one injected deformation.
The unfolding recovers a known +30 % Gaussian bump injected into pseudo-data in truth Eavail
(center 0.3 GeV, width 0.15 GeV). The recovered step-2 mean weight is 1.048 against the injected
1.0485. Residuals against reweighted truth on the Eavail -marginal have median 0.9999 and standard
deviation 0.0006 (0.06 %); the per-3D-bin residual standard deviation is 3.2 % from MC statistics
in sparse high-recoil corners. The closure’s marginal scatter is far below the 3.7 % data-versus-2D
scatter. Because the closure uses configuration (a) and the data comparison sets (e) against (f),
the ratio of the two scatters is not quoted. Total normalization matches to better than 1 %. In
the metric of §5 (Eavail projection) the residual is 1.84 % against 18.1 % injected (ratio 0.102). No
acceptance threshold set before the test is documented, so it has no pass or fail verdict.
The test covers one shape, this Gaussian, injected into the pseudo-data and unfolded with the same
detector response that the unfolding uses. For this injection the configuration-(a) estimator neither
removes the injected feature nor creates a spurious one. That result is shown for configuration (a) only
and does not extend to (e); since the data-versus-2D scatter sets (e) against (f), it is not interpreted as
data–simulation structure resolved by the available-energy axis. The test does not exclude estimator
bias for other deformations (§5.1). The hidden-variable closure exceeds its threshold at the largest
amplitude (§5, App. D).

7.5

Classifier-family cross-check (GBDT vs. NN)

Our gradient-boosted decision trees differ from the neural-network classifiers used in published
OmniFold analyses [1, 18]. This cross-check uses the configuration-(a) GBDT unfold, with puritybased subtraction. The weight w = p/(1 − p) holds only if the GBDT output is a calibrated posterior.
The step-1 reco classifier (data vs. MC reco) has a near-diagonal reliability curve, Brier ≈ 0.25 at
AUC ≈ 0.537. Data and reco MC are nearly indistinguishable, so the reweight is a modest correction.
A full OmniFold cross-check with a Keras multilayer perceptron (MLP), the same weighted 3D
inputs and the same five-iteration loop gives a total ratio of 1.0078 to the GBDT result and median
projection differences of 0.7 % to 1.4 % (Fig. 18). In the populated bulk the configuration-(a) result
therefore agrees between the two tested classifier families.

7.6

Generator comparison in 3D

The published ancillary files provide only binned 2D model histograms. Quantitative 3D comparisons,
or comparisons in arbitrary projections, require generating truth-level events and histogramming
them as d3 σ in the same fiducial phase space with the same truth Eavail definition. Because the
unfolded result is at truth level, the comparisons need no detector simulation. The GENIE, NuWro
and GiBUU samples were generated and processed for this analysis, and MINERvA Tune v1 was
extracted from the analysis simulation’s truth with its full tune weight; no spectra were digitized or
externally supplied.
• GENIE 2.12.10 central value, generated with gevgen on a CH target with the MINERvA
ME FHC flux, using the DefaultPlusValenciaMEC cross-section splines [4] but gevgen’s default
event-generator list, which excludes MEC, so the sample has no 2p2h events (§7.7). It carries
no MINERvA reweights, and its events are reweighted to the analysis flux as described below.
• MINERvA Tune v1, the analysis tune (base GENIE + RPA, low-recoil 2p2h, and nonresonant-pion reweights), taken directly from the truth of the analysis simulation, which
reproduces the normalization of the published ancillary curve to 0.01 %.
• NuWro 21.09 [6], an independent generator on C with the same flux (after reweighting below)
and phase space.
• GiBUU 2019 [7], a transport-theory generator on C, built and run natively, without a
container, with the same flux and phase space.
NEUT [43] is not included.

Flux repair and normalization. The GENIE (CV and +Valencia 2p2h) and NuWro samples
were generated from a flux that had no PPFX weights, was capped at 50 GeV, and stored flux
densities in variable-width bins that were sampled and normalized as contents, which under-weighted
every wide high-energy bin and suppressed the high-p∥ predictions and the integrated rates (Ref. [37],
R1.3). The repair reweights each event exactly to the analysis PPFX central-value flux on 0–100 GeV,
with correctly sampled 50–100 GeV supplements. The repaired GENIE and NuWro predictions are
used in §§7–8, except in three studies, flagged where they appear, that keep the unrepaired samples:
the FSI-dial studies, the departure ratio of §9.2 and the NuWro-shaped prior of §12. GiBUU reads
its own flux and receives an energy-dependent per-event reweight to the same convention, which
changes its integrated cross section by a factor 0.9966. Its sample stops at Eν ≈ 20 GeV, so it is
empty above p∥ ≈ 20 GeV/c. The analysis simulation, MINERvA Tune v1, is not affected. Each
prediction is normalized by its own flux-averaged total CC cross section per nucleon.
Figure 19 shows the comparison. Totals over the full measurement phase space follow GiBUU
(2.22 × 10−38 ) < NuWro (2.66 × 10−38 ) < Tune v1 (2.71 × 10−38 ) < GENIE CV (2.76 × 10−38 )
< data (3.07 × 10−38 cm2 /nucleon). All four under-predict the rate by 10 % to 28 % of the data.
Integrating the reported Eavail range with the catch bin dropped gives the same order. All four closely
track the pT and p∥ projections but differ in Eavail : NuWro and Tune v1 suppress the lowest-Eavail
(quasielastic) bin below the untuned GENIE CV through RPA and nuclear effects, while GiBUU
does not. Data lie above all four across the quasielastic–∆ dip, 0.1 ≤ Eavail < 0.4 GeV (in the lowest
bin GiBUU reaches the data). Of the displayed projections, only Eavail locates this excess; the 2D
(pT , p∥ ) measurement does not resolve it.

from GENIE 2.12 CV, MINERvA Tune v1, NuWro 21.09, and GiBUU 2019 (GENIE and NuWro:

of the five-dimensional covariance of §9.6 would supply a 3D covariance; it has been computed but is
not quoted (§7) or used for any result in this note.

7.7

The low-available-energy excess and the known low-recoil deficit

We compare the low-Eavail excess with candidate model changes, both by reweighting and by
generating the relevant physics. MINERvA first isolated the rate enhancement between quasielastic

and ∆ peaks, with multi-nucleon final states, in a low-three-momentum-transfer subsample [5]. The
empirical tune was built to absorb it; our central values in muon kinematics and Eavail show the
predictions falling short in the same region.
The tested pion-FSI variations do not account for it. Reweighting 2M central-value GENIE
events with GENIE’s grwght1p changes dσ/dEavail by less than a percent for both tested pionFSI dials. FrInel_pi shifts the spectrum by ≤ 0.74 % (peak in 0.10–0.20 GeV) and FrAbs_pi by
≤ 0.82 % (peak in the lowest bin). Both are ratios within the unrepaired GENIE sample (§7.6), in
which the flux defect cancels to first order. Neither was recomputed. The integrated rate moves
by ±0.02–0.03 % at ±1σ. 1 A dial that moves the spectrum by less than 1 % cannot account
for an excess of 8 % to 36 % per bin over the GENIE central value below Eavail = 0.4 GeV. This
conclusion is limited to these dials and their range; other transport models or proton-FSI effects are
not excluded. Recent MINERvA quasielastic-like measurements point toward overestimated proton
and charged-pion FSI in their model comparisons [44]. Their different signal definition prevents
directly identifying that effect with our inclusive discrepancy.
Comparison with a missing 2p2h component. The base GENIE central value uses the default
event-generator list and contains no meson-exchange-current (2p2h/MEC) events. None of the
1.48 × 106 CC events of the generated sample is a MEC event, and the 50–100 GeV supplement is
MEC-free as well. Before the flux reweight of §7.6, the sample’s unweighted modes are QE 11 %,
RES 22 %, DIS 66 % and COH 0.9 %. 2p2h must therefore be added ; reweighting cannot supply
it. The mode decomposition multiplies each bin of the central-value dσ/dEavail by the fraction of
its events in each mode. Overlaid with data, it places the largest positive data−CV gaps in the
quasielastic–∆ dip, [0.10, 0.20) and [0.20, 0.40). The CV is much closer to data in the QE-dominated
[0, 0.10) and [0.40, 1.50) regions. At central value, 69 % of the positive deficit lies at Eavail ≤ 0.4 GeV,
where 2p2h events populate. Closing the integrated deficit requires 2p2h at ≈ 45 % of the QE rate
(68 % to 71 % in the two dip bins). This is the standard magnitude of the empirical and Valencia
MEC models; the FSI shifts are below one percent (Fig. 20).
GENIE with Valencia 2p2h enabled. We regenerated the GENIE central value with Valencia
2p2h enabled (–event-generator-list Default+CCMEC, 2M events). The Nieves–Simo–Vacas MEC
channel [46, 47] is present in the splines but absent from the total-CC cross-section graph written by
GENIE’s gspl2root, so we normalize by the non-MEC CC count: QE+RES+DIS+COH is fixed to
the known total, and MEC is added through 1/(1 − fMEC ) with fMEC = 2.87 %. 2p2h falls in the
dip and brings all three low-Eavail bins toward data (Fig. 21). In central values, it closes 52 % of the
data−CV gap at Eavail ≤ 0.4 GeV and 63 % of the integrated deficit (CV −7.2 % → −2.7 %). The
prediction still falls short in the dip, especially [0.10, 0.20), but its integral over the reported Eavail
range is closer to data than Tune v1 (−2.7 % against −9.5 %). Tune v1 adds MINERvA’s empirical
low-recoil 2p2h enhancement and RPA to the stock Valencia MEC model. The central values are
consistent with the known need for that enhancement, and with the NuWro and GiBUU predictions,
whose native 2p2h is not enhanced, also falling below the data in the dip; they do not establish that
need.
2p2h leaves the high-Eavail [1.50, 3.00) bin unchanged. There, data exceed the GENIE CV by
about 10 % (data/CV 1.10) and exceed Tune v1 as well; the high-Eavail excess over Tune v1 is
1

FrInel_pi is disabled in the standard MAT-MINERvA set of systematics. This is MINERvA’s current practice
(private communication from the collaboration, 2026), and the reason is a degeneracy (of overlapping pion-FSI dials,
one must be dropped), not a defect of that dial.

localized on the (Eavail , W ) plane in §8.
The generator’s 2p2h event category is also not the same as a two-body current: recent NEUT
studies, for example, include two-body currents that contribute to one-particle–one-hole final states
and examine their interplay with axial form factors [48]. Visible-energy comparisons can, moreover,
depend on both the primary interaction and the subsequent hadronic transport.
Ascencio et al. [8] report the same deficit in MINERvA’s dedicated low-recoil inclusive
2
d σ/(dq3 dEavail ) measurement for q3 < 1.2 GeV at the same ⟨Eν ⟩ ∼ 6 GeV, finding GENIE and
NuWro underpredicting their low-Eavail data without enhanced 2p2h. Our muon-kinematics
extension integrates over all q3 instead of restricting q3 , which limits the comparison to the shape and
direction in Eavail , without absolute normalization. In those terms our central values are consistent
with that established low-recoil deficit. A bin-identical comparison of central values is in §7.8.

7.8

Fourth axis: three-momentum transfer q3

The fourth axis adds one feature (§7), three-momentum transfer q3 = |⃗q |, chosen because its
(Eavail , q3 ) plane separates quasielastic, 2p2h and resonance processes and permits bin-compatible
comparison to MINERvA’s dedicated low-recoil d2 σ/dq3 dEavail measurement (Ascencio et al. [8]).
Truth q3 comes from the generator (CVUniverse::Getq3True()); reconstructed q3 uses the muon Q2
and the calorimetric recoil (CVUniverse::RecoQ3()). Both are evaluated for all twelve playlists, as
for Eavail . Lateral systematic shifts change q3 but leave Eavail unchanged, so a shifted q3 is stored for
each lateral universe instead of reusing the central value. The classifier inputs are (pT , p∥ , Eavail , q3 ),
with deliberately coarse q3 binning [0, 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.2, 2.0, 100] GeV (seven bins including a final
catch bin), giving 14 × 16 × 7 × 7 = 10 976 four-dimensional bins (4830 populated).
Marginalization check (4D→3D). In configuration (a), the q3 marginalization reproduces the
3D Eavail spectrum and low-recoil excess (Fig. 23, right). The Eavail -marginal data/Tune v1 ratio is

1.21 in the lowest bin, dips to 1.01 across the quasielastic–∆ region, and rises to 1.16–1.23 in the
high-Eavail DIS tail (integrated data/Tune v1 = 1.13). These ratios are to Tune v1, whereas §7.7
uses untuned GENIE. Recovering the 3D result as a by-product of the higher-dimensional unfolding
is the 4D→3D analog of the 3D→2D marginalization check (§7.3).
The (Eavail , q3 ) plane. The (Eavail , q3 ) plane (Fig. 23, left) shows structure that the q3 marginal,
integrated over Eavail , does not resolve; the marginal’s data/Tune v1 ratio is 1.03–1.19 (center). At
fixed Eavail in the quasielastic–∆ dip, data/Tune v1 rises with q3 . The central value over-predicts
low-q3 , moderate-Eavail cells (ratio down to 0.66); data excess concentrates at low Eavail (all q3 )
and toward high q3 . The muon-only or fixed-q3 projections considered here do not resolve this
correlation. It bears on the bin-compatible comparison with Ascencio et al. The map’s comparator
is the MINERvA Tune v1 prior, distinct from the untuned GENIE sample of §7.7.
No 4D uncertainty. Neither a 4D uncertainty nor a ratio of the covariance from simultaneous
variation of every reweightable band and the flux multisim (the unified throw of §4.5) to the block
covariance is reported. A corrected 4D covariance would use the actual minus and plus variation
endpoints; MAT’s mean-centered covariance blocks, normalized by 1/N ; one fixed estimator seed;
and mean-centered joint throws, with the mean shift reported separately. It would not subtract a
scalar jitter floor. Because the single-pass shifted observables exist only for events selected at the
central value, re-selected event loops (§4.7) would be needed to bound migration effects.
Bin-identical comparison to the published low-recoil measurement. Supplemental data
from Ref. [8] (44-cell d2 σ/(dEavail dq3 ) with its full covariance, distributed inside the public arXiv
source package) permit a direct quantitative cross-check. Both measurements are merged onto their
maximal common grid, the edges present in both binnings. Our (pT , p∥ ) marginalization is restricted
to p∥ < 20 GeV/c to mirror the Ascencio muon selection (θµ < 20◦ , 1.5 < pµ < 20 GeV). Together,
these give two super-cells, Eavail < 0.4 GeV in q3 ∈ [0.4, 0.6) and [0.6, 1.2) GeV: the low-recoil 2p2h
region. Our central result lies 9.2 % and 6.0 % above Ascencio. No pulls or full-covariance χ2 are
reported (§7; Fig. 22). Both share MINERvA flux/detector systematics, treated here as independent.
The pµ and p∥ cuts differ by ≲ 6 % kinematic smearing at the 20 GeV boundary. The two analyses
also define true Eavail differently (§7.1): in this analysis’s simulation, Ascencio’s broader definition
moves 11.0 % of the population of our lowest true-Eavail bin (Eavail < 0.1 GeV) out of it. That shift
has the sign and location of the residual. The two common cells, however, span our three lowest bins
(Eavail < 0.4 GeV), and the aggregate shift over those bins has not been computed. The definition
difference therefore remains an alternative explanation that is not excluded. At the 10 % scale of
the residual, the comparison neither refutes nor validates the result. A comparison on all 44 cells
has not been made; it requires re-unfolding on the Ascencio edges, which is straightforward for the
unbinned method, and a systematic sweep on that binning.

Figure 23: 4D data/Tune v1 ratio (“data/GENIE” in the figure) projected onto (Eavail , q3 ) (left; blank

8

Fifth axis: W and the (Eavail , W ) plane

For the central values, Valencia 2p2h closes about half of the low-recoil gap (§7.7) but leaves a separate
excess over the MINERvA Tune v1 prior at high Eavail . There the predicted 2p2h contribution is
zero, and the two tested pion final-state-interaction variations change the prediction by less than
one percent, too little to explain the excess.
GiBUU is low throughout the Eavail tail. Its W -integrated five-dimensional data/generator ratio
is 1.59 at Eavail ∈ [0.8, 1.5) and 1.37 at [1.5, 3.0), in configuration (a), the LightGBM unfolding
with purity-based subtraction (§3.2); its sample stops at Eν ≈ 20 GeV (§8.5). GENIE CV instead
matches data at [0.8, 1.5) and is low at [1.5, 3.0), where its three-dimensional data/CV ratio is 1.10
(§7.7) in configuration (e), the deterministic learner with refined negative-weight subtraction.
Adding the hadronic invariant mass W as the fifth OmniFold axis maps where in W the high52

Eavail excess lies. The validation of §9.1 includes a model-dependence study; it does not establish
a bias–variance tradeoff across estimator settings and provides no new total uncertainty for this
projection.

8.1

W as a fifth axis

p
Truth W is the experimenter’s invariant mass W = M 2 + 2M (Eν − Eµ ) − Q2 , with struck-nucleon
mass M . Reconstructed W combines the calorimetric energy transfer q0 of the reconstructed-q3
estimator and the muon-kinematic Q2 with the average nucleon mass. The external generator
predictions instead compute truth W with the neutron mass for every event and place events with
W 2 ≤ 0 in the lowest W bin, whereas the analysis uses the struck-nucleon mass and excludes
such events from its five-observable truth sample. In the GENIE and NuWro samples this moves
0.14–0.15% of the events into another W bin. In GENIE CV and NuWro (without their 50–100 GeV
samples) it changes the coarse-cell predictions by about 0.1 % at the median and at most 2.2 %. In
any cell, that is at most about one Monte Carlo standard error of the prediction. The change was
not evaluated at cell level for GENIE+MEC and cannot be evaluated for GiBUU.
Like q3 (§7.8), and unlike Eavail , W changes under the lateral systematic shifts, so a shifted W is
stored for each lateral universe. The event-loop output files also store truth hadronic diagnostics:
the proton and pion multiplicities and the polar angle of the summed momentum of all final-state
particles other than muons and neutrinos. The W edges [0, 1.1, 1.4, 1.8, 2.2, 3.0, 100] GeV bracket
the quasielastic peak, the ∆, the transition region and the DIS tail.
The configuration-(a) five-dimensional unfolding in (pT , p∥ , Eavail , q3 , W ) agrees under marginalization with the lower-dimensional results: integrating over W reproduces the configuration-(a) 4D
normalization to 0.11 % (5D/4D total = 1.0011) with median differences per axis of 0.3 % to 1.5 %,
and dσ/dW is finite and non-negative in all six bins.

8.2

Implemented definitions of the five observables

Table 10 gives the implemented truth and reconstructed definitions of the five unfolded observables,
as used in the companion journal article. Eµ , pµ , and θµ are the muon energy, momentum,
and angle; q0 is the calorimetric estimate of the energy transfer; M is the struck-nucleon mass,
M̄ = (1.5Mn + Mp )/2.5. Exact Q2 is −(k − k ′ )2 from the generator’s four-vectors; truth W instead
uses the massless-muon Q2 with θµ from the beam axis, a difference whose effect on W is given
below.
Reconstructed q3 and W combine the muon kinematics with the calorimetric estimate q0 of
2
2
the energy transfer, which is separate from reconstructed Eavail . Since Wreco
= (M̄ + q0 )2 − q3,reco
and q3,reco increases with q0 at fixed muon kinematics, reconstructed W is an exact function of
(pT , p∥ , q3 ), apart from the clamp at W 2 = 0, which applies to 1.5 % of simulated events.
At truth level, q3 is computed from the true neutrino and muon four-vectors, but θµ , and with it
pT and p∥ , is measured from a fixed beam axis tilted by 58.87 mrad from the detector axis rather
than from the true neutrino direction. Truth W is therefore fixed by (pT , p∥ , q3 ) only up to that
event-by-event angle, of about 1 mrad, and the choice of M : the residual is within ±2 MeV for 68%
of events and within −49 to +67 MeV for 99.9%; 0.25 % of events (weighted) change their coarse W
bin, and 0.15 % have no real solution.
The five reconstructed inputs therefore share information and do not, by themselves, provide
five independent constraints: the result is five simultaneous observables, not five independent
measurements. The companion journal article distinguishes the five classifier inputs, the lowerdimensional support they populate, and the five reported observables. Only reconstructed W is

The analysis excludes events with negative truth W 2 , a weight fraction of 4.9 × 10−5 , from its
five-observable truth sample and normalization; §8.1 gives the external predictions’ convention. The
generators measure pT and p∥ about their own neutrino axis; their pT differs from the analysis truth
pT by ±4 MeV for 68% of events. Over the 109 joint-test cells, this convention changes the analysis
simulation’s cell contents by factors between 0.9965 and 1.0035 (median 1.000008), at the level of its
Monte Carlo precision, and shifts GENIE’s central prediction by between −0.47 and +0.53 of each
cell’s null standard deviation. For GENIE with MEC, NuWro and GiBUU its effect is not reliably
determined (Ref. [37], R1.18).

8.4

Distribution of the excess in the (Eavail , W ) plane

Figure 24 shows the unfolded five-dimensional result projected onto (Eavail , W ), with the MINERvA
Tune v1 prediction subtracted. That prediction is the OmniFold prior, the simulation’s truth weighted
by its full tune weight; the untuned GENIE CV of §8.5 is a separate prediction. In cell-integrated
terms, the positive excess lies mostly at high Eavail and high W . The two highest-Eavail bands
carry 57 % of it, and the region Eavail ≥ 0.8 GeV carries 67 %, with 83 % of that at W ≥ 1.8 GeV.
The largest cell, the corner Eavail > 3 GeV, W > 3 GeV, holds 22 %. A secondary excess lies at
W < 1.1 GeV, with a small deficit at W ≃ 1.4–1.8 GeV.
These statements hold under the nominal detector response, and the regularization bias of Sec. 9
is largest at high Eavail and high W . Estimator-seed shifts of central values are a small fraction of
the projected uncertainties (§9.6). The seed sensitivity of the shares, which are ratios of central-value
differences, has not been measured separately. The response-mismatch closure of Appendix D is
designed to test whether an incorrect hadronic response can move strength into or out of the low-W
component. It has not yet produced a result.

panel.

8.5

Comparison with four external generators

All four external generators fall below the data in the high-Eavail , high-W corner. Figure 25 compares
the (Eavail , W ) predictions of GENIE CV, GENIE+Valencia 2p2h/MEC, NuWro and GiBUU. As in
§7.6, each is normalized by its flux-averaged total CC cross section per nucleon, and those of GENIE

and NuWro are flux-corrected. The 3D and (Eavail , W ) comparisons use the same five-dimensional
histogram of each generator. GENIE CV totals 2.76 × 10−38 cm2 /nucleon in the measurement phase
space in both; GENIE+MEC uses the normalization convention of the 3D comparison.
In the corner (Eavail ≥ 0.8 GeV, W ≥ 1.8 GeV), data/generator = 1.14, 1.14, 1.16 and 1.61,
respectively. For GENIE and NuWro the corner ratios exceed their integrated ratios (1.11, 1.07
and 1.15) by 1 % to 7 %, compared with 16 % for GiBUU, whose integrated ratio is 1.39 (σ =
2.22 × 10−38 cm2 /nucleon, against 3.07 × 10−38 for the data).
GiBUU’s larger contrast comes from its sample’s energy limit: it was generated only up to
Eν ≈ 20 GeV (§7.6). In GENIE and NuWro, neutrinos above this energy carry 14.3 % to 14.8 %
of the corner cross section and 6.8 % to 7.4 % overall. Removing them raises these generators’
corner-to-integrated contrast to 9 % to 16 %, bracketing GiBUU’s. If GiBUU is instead supplied with
the shares that GENIE and NuWro carry above this energy, data/GiBUU becomes about 1.37 in the
corner and 1.29 integrated. With the GENIE and NuWro fluxes corrected and GiBUU supplied with
the shares above this energy, each generator’s central-value corner ratio lies close to its integrated
ratio. GiBUU is lower than the others everywhere.
Valencia 2p2h raises the GENIE+MEC integrated prediction while leaving the corner essentially
unchanged (1.142 → 1.139), since it populates low W . At W ∈ [2.2, 3.0) GeV, data/generator = 1.12,
1.11, 1.13 and 1.34, again close to the integrated ratios. At 1.4 ≤ W < 1.8 GeV, instead, both
GENIE predictions lie 9 % to 12 % above the data. These are central-value ratios. No compatibility
metric based on the configuration-(a) five-axis covariance is quoted, because the estimator-seed
sensitivity of its construction, measured with two companion covariances, exceeds the limit set before
production (§9.6); §10.7 gives a sixteen-cell χ2 comparison.
The localization in §8.4 is relative to MINERvA Tune v1; data exceed all four external generators
both overall and in the corner.

Figure 25: Projections of the unfolded (Eavail , W ) cross section onto Eavail (left) and W (right), with
the predictions of four generators (GENIE CV, GENIE+MEC, NuWro, GiBUU; GENIE and NuWro

MINERvA shallow-inelastic neutrino and antineutrino measurements also report shape and

normalization disagreements with generators [49]. They motivate tests across the resonance–DIS
transition, but their selections and reported observables differ from this inclusive projection, so
they provide physics context, not an independent confirmation of the same localized excess. A later
significance for the location identified in the central-value map must account for any selection of the
tested region made with these data. The published low-recoil cross-check in (Eavail , q3 ) (§7.8) gives
central values 9.2 % and 6.0 % above the published measurement on the maximal common grid.

9

Five-dimensional estimator: validation and uncertainty

This section validates the five-dimensional estimator behind the central values of §§7–8. It covers what
each test isolates (§9.1), background subtraction and generator-sized truth changes (§9.2), why the
estimator does not follow such changes (§9.3), model dependence and the reporting definitions it rules
out (§9.4), the coverage test (§9.5) and the status of the five-axis covariance of the configuration-(a)
central value (§9.6). The sixteen-cell result of configuration (e) follows in §10, and the calibrated
generator tests built on a variant of this estimator in §11. The unbinned negative-weight background
method itself is derived in App. C.

9.1

Which source of error is being tested

Prior reweighting changes the frequencies of existing simulated truth events while retaining their
detector-response simulation; a response mismatch instead changes how a given truth is reconstructed.
Finite classifier capacity and iteration count approximate the inverse and can leave residuals in either
case. Coverage asks whether the resulting intervals contain their specified target at the stated rate
(§9.5). These mechanisms are separate: an incorrect generator prediction alone does not diagnose an
interval failure. Each test below isolates one intervention.
All 2D closures in §5 unfold reweighted simulated signal without background, so they do not test
background subtraction.
Table 12 lists the validation studies of the article and of this note (detector response: §5.1 and
§11.2). Letters refer to Table 2.

9.2

Background subtraction and generator-sized truth changes

Background subtraction. End-to-end five-dimensional pseudo-experiments add backgroundsimulation events and rebuild per-bin purity weights as in the measurement. At nominal truth they
return the highest-W column of (Eavail , W ) 3.5 % to 4.1 % low and the highest-Eavail , highest-W cell
1.7 % high; without background, the same experiments recover the truth to 0.22 %. Relative to these
cells’ total uncertainty in the configuration-(a) covariance, the bias is 0.36–1.07 standard deviations.
The bias comes from the purity-based subtraction (§C), enters no quoted covariance, and has no
established size on data. Refined unbinned negative-weight subtraction (App. C), applied with a
deterministic variant of the five-dimensional LightGBM production estimator and a refinement of
100 trees and 8 leaves, brings the highest-W column to 0.11 % to 0.24 %, the corner cell to 0.24 %
and every other (Eavail , W ) cell to at most 0.39 % over sixty nominal-truth experiments, which are
not paired with the purity-based ones (Fig. 37). Some joint cells remain up to 1.0 % high, up to 14
standard errors, against −0.2 % without background; the mechanism is not established. On data,
the two treatments differ by a median of 0.36 % and at most 1.4 % over the reported functionals.
These differences measure method sensitivity, not bias. Negative-weight subtraction is not used for
the configuration-(a) five-dimensional central value; the sixteen-cell result uses it (§10).

Generator-sized truth changes. The unfolding does not follow a GiBUU-to-GENIE Eavail shape
reweighting of the truth (0.70–1.33 per Eavail bin, total preserved). The GENIE prediction behind
the ratio uses a mis-sampled flux (§7.6), so the ratio is a valid known-truth deformation of generator
size but not a measured GiBUU–GENIE difference. The (Eavail , W ) residuals have a median of 9.7 %;
they reach 74 % in the highest-W column and several percent in the largest cells. The 74 % residual
is in the cell 0.8 ≤ Eavail < 1.5 GeV, W ≥ 3 GeV, whose share of the cross section the deformation
study did not record. A q3 deformation preserving the (Eavail , W ) truth shifts these cells by up
to 37 %. Signal-only experiments with the same seeds reproduce both results. The residuals are
therefore a property of the estimator (five iterations, the GBDT configuration of §3), not of the
background treatment; their status in the quoted uncertainties is given in §9.3.

9.3

Why the estimator does not follow a generator-sized change

Both OmniFold steps behave as intended: the first matches the reconstructed pseudo-data within
statistical noise, and the second reproduces the truth-level weight sums to 0.5 %. Yet the unfolded
result folds back onto the data less well than the true model does. With noise-free pseudo-data the
unfolding recovers more than 99 % of the change that the departure produces in the reconstructed
distribution, whereas the median (Eavail , W ) truth residual ranges from 5 % to 11 %, with 74 % in
the worst cell. The unrecovered reconstructed-level difference is small relative to the departure but
statistically significant at the analysis exposure.
With noise-free pseudo-data, thirty iterations reduce the Eavail residual by 26 %; the Eavail
residual continues to fall slowly, while the q3 residual grows with iterations. A larger classifier
improves the reconstructed-level fit without reducing the Eavail truth residual, and removing the
regression for events that fail reconstruction does not reduce that residual either. No single cause is
established, and no estimator change within the scope of the study meets the recovery requirement,
set before the test, for the GiBUU-to-GENIE departure. The Eavail and q3 residuals, a regularization
(unfolding-model) bias of the estimator, enter no uncertainty quoted for the configuration-(a) central

value. For the sixteen-cell result (§10), the model term of the reported covariance is the change in
the unfolded data when the prior is reweighted to each generator (GENIE + MEC, NuWro, GiBUU;
§10.3). This term is unscaled and is not a calibrated bound. The fake-data study of §10.5 measures
the estimator’s bias and compares it with the reported covariance. The pseudo-experiments of the
paper’s recovery figure, sixty for the development variant at the GiBUU-to-GENIE Eavail shape and
twenty for each of three shapes not used in the joint-test estimator’s selection, use seed sets that
were not held out from the estimator’s development.
Comparison with exact binned unfolding. A diagnostic in simulation compares the boostedtree residual with that of an exact binned iterative unfolding. The binned unfolding unfolds the
same simulated events, with their exact efficiency and the response binned in the 10,499 occupied
five-dimensional reconstructed cells, starting from the nominal prior. The comparison uses noise-free
signal-only pseudo-data, the 109 supported fine joint cells (J cells) and the fixed historical truth
deformations D1, W1, W3 and the q3 stress, pooled with W2 (all defined in §9.4). The boosted-tree
comparator for W2 includes background; the outcome is unchanged without it. A residual is
eligible when its diagnosis is stable under the truth resolution: of the 376 J-cell and deformation
combinations with residuals above 2 %, 232 changed classification with the truth resolution or the
response weighting and were excluded, leaving 144 (38 % of the 376). At five iterations the J-cell
medians of the absolute residual agree: 9.5 %, 5.4 %, 5.6 % and 4.3 % for the boosted trees, against
8.3 %, 5.4 %, 4.9 % and 5.3 % for the exact binned unfolding, for D1, W1, W3 and the q3 stress. Of
the 144 eligible residuals, pooled over the deformations, 15 are dominated by the tree approximation.
Only 2 of these 15 lie in identified quantities (as defined in the diagnostic record cited below), a
share of 0.014 of the 144. Of the 144, 121 lie in quantities that the binned response constrains weakly
at the analysis exposure. Their Cramér–Rao widths exceed 10 % at both truth resolutions tested
(median 31 % at the coarser resolution, that of the J cells themselves), so the regularization trades a
large variance for the bias. Longer noise-free exact iteration removes much of the residual for D1 (J
median 3.3 % after 200 iterations and 0.8 % after 105 ) and part of it for W1 (3.3 % and 2.8 %). It
does not do so for W3 and the q3 stress (4.4 % and 6.6 % after 200 iterations, 8.9 % and 6.7 % after
105 ), whose within-cell binning bias grows with the iteration number. The long runs do not converge
(only 9–34 of the 109 J quantities converge for each traced deformation), so these values are last
iterates. The coarser muon projection retained only three cases and is uninformative. Within this
comparison, the residual is consistent with the regularization of the inverse problem at this binning
and prior, and the tree approximation is not supported as its main cause. This conclusion holds
for a binned, signal-only and noise-free comparison at the simulation’s detector response, on the
development truths, and for these 144 residuals only. It does not address what unbinned or biased
estimators could recover, the coverage failure of §9.5 or the validity of any interval, and it establishes
no impossibility. The precision thresholds that the diagnostic proposes are not acceptance criteria
for any result in this note. The exclusions, definitions and numerical verification are in the binned
response diagnostic record (Ref. [37], R1.14).
Higher-capacity background refinement and data stability. The refinement classifier underfits, which causes the residual background bias (App. C). A candidate with raised refinement
capacity, configuration (e) of the sixteen-cell result (§10), passes the nominal background-inclusive
closure on 40 fresh pseudo-experiments (largest deviation 2.7 standard errors) and a pooled test of
its statistical intervals (pull width 1.02). The pooled test does not establish coverage and width
for every functional. The candidate fails the data-stability test, whose criterion was set before it
was run: input perturbations within floating-point rounding move functionals by a median of 0.8

and at most 2.6 statistical standard deviations. The baseline estimator’s shifts, a median of 0.8
and at most 2.5, place this instability in the estimator family. The instability also explains the
difference of at most 1.2 % on data between the driver and the development pipeline. The data
bootstrap, which refits the full chain per replica, already includes this rounding-level variability:
each replica carries the same spread (median 0.23 % per joint cell), about 37 % of the bootstrap
variance and below a tenth of any total uncertainty. Central values are therefore reproducible only
to within this spread. The paired perturbation study of §9.4 tests whether the bootstrap contains
this variability, for this candidate only. A declared probe on simulated pseudo-data of two-fold
cross-fitting and of two tree regularisations found no variant that met the data-stability requirement,
so the estimator is unchanged. A declared study of the mechanism on one pseudo-experiment
found that the perturbation reaches the estimator by breaking ties among equal float32 values,
which changes LightGBM’s histogram bins; a perturbation that keeps the ties removes the spread.
Perturbing only the refinement stage’s input carries the full spread, so the refinement is a sufficient
entry. Neither finding is a statement about the data’s spread, and equal float32 values are what
float32 storage of these kinematics produces, not a defect of the inputs.
Unfolding-model dependence. For historical shapes held out while this candidate was assessed
(NuWro; GENIE with enhanced meson-exchange currents), its unfolding-model dependence is median
2 % to 6 % and at most 16 % to 31 %, within the configuration-(a) total uncertainty in 20 of the 42
(Eavail , W ) cells. No uncertainty from this assessment is used for a quoted result.

9.4

Model dependence, variability and reporting resolution

The scalar five-dimensional estimator studied here uses production LightGBM.
These studies measure three different quantities: truth-dependent responses to iterations and
capacity, small repeat variability at one setting, and appreciable prior sensitivity. Unfolding
benchmarks also assess these quantities separately [50]. The studies do not establish a bias–variance
tradeoff across estimator settings, and regularization bias can invalidate intervals based solely on
estimator variability [51]; neither reference supplies the variance measurement that is missing for
this estimator.
The noise-free study unfolds the full simulation’s reweighted reconstructed events without
background. Baseline classifiers and the missed-event regressor use 100 trees and 8 leaves; the
capacity probe uses 400 trees and 31 leaves. Repeated experiments use estimator R, the raisedrefinement-capacity candidate of §9.3: five iterations, baseline OmniFold estimators and a separate
400-tree, 31-leaf background-refinement classifier. With seed 42, its 40 nominal experiments and
20 at each of five departures include Poisson pseudo-data, changing MC splits and background
refinement. These experiments are not an independent validation: their samples were used during
development, and their assessment seeds, although separate, were later also used in the estimator’s
development.
Saved fine-grid outputs are integrated into the supported J, H2 and EW cells of the table below.
Unsupported cells remain in the full-domain rate. The interval assessment keeps all 42 EW cells,
including the three that the support rule excludes (definitions and normalization checks in App. B.2).

The truth labels denote fixed historical deformations, built before the generator flux was repaired:
D1 is a GiBUU/GENIE Eavail ratio, W1 a NuWro/GENIE Eavail ratio, W2 a GENIE MEC/CV

others corrected generator comparisons. The q3 stress changes the conditional distribution at fixed
(Eavail , W ) with amplitude 0.3.
Recovery changes, but not uniformly. Figure 26 shows the traces over the recorded iteration
ranges (later checkpoint entries are excluded, App. B.2); none is extrapolated. D1 and W1 improve
slowly. W3’s J-cell median absolute residual rises from 5.57 % at iteration 5 to 6.57 % at 200, and
its maximum from 32.9 % to 44.8 %. The corresponding H2 medians are 3.42 % and 3.68 %. The
persistence of the W3 residual is established over this finite scan only, not for unlimited iterations or
for every possible classifier. The q3 stress shows how much the functional matters: its EW median
residual rises from 4.99 % at iteration 5 to 6.42 % at 30 even while some joint-cell summaries improve.
The capacity probe changes recovery. At five iterations, the most the computing budget allowed
at that capacity, the larger classifiers lower the largest residual proxy over the development truths
(lower row of Fig. 26) by 3% on J and 8% on H2, short of the improvement of at least 20% required
before the larger capacity would be used. The joint-test estimator keeps five iterations under a rule
fixed before the comparison. The rule would have replaced five iterations by 10, 15 or 20 only if one
of these lowered its measure of model dependence, the largest residual proxy over the development
truth changes, on the J or the H2 cells. None did. Because the measure is a maximum over the truth
changes, the slow gains for D1 and W1 do not lower the measure: on H2, over the noise-free traces
of Fig. 26, it is 0.314 at five iterations and at least 0.384 at 10, 15 or 20. The sixteen-cell result is
reported on the same H2 grid after a purity merge of its supported boxes, with total uncertainties
(§10.1).
Reporting definitions for a five-dimensional measurement. The five-dimensional precision
study assessed three reporting definitions at the development resolution: the 109 J cells (RD1),
the 27 H2 cells (RD2) and the 39 EW cells (RD3). Its bounded unfolding-model component, the
largest shift over five generator-sized truth departures at the smallest envelope the vertices allow, has
median 12.0 %, 9.6 % and 13.4 % respectively (90th percentile 38.6, 22.5 and 39.2 %), with GiBUU
the dominant vertex in most cells. The study set its total-width target, a median 68% half-width of
at most 10 %, and its selection procedure before it was run. The study adds the model component
linearly to the probabilistic half-width, cell by cell. The model component alone therefore exceeds
the total-width target in RD1 and RD3, and in RD2 the target is exceeded once the flat 1.4 %
normalization is added (median at least 11.0 %). The model-component target, a median of at most
5 %, and the recovery target, recovery of at least 75% of a generator-sized departure (a residual
proxy of Fig. 26 of at most 0.25, against 0.31–0.63 measured), fail in every definition. The selection
procedure then takes RD3 as the fallback, and RD3 fails the total-width target. None of the three

selection procedure permits within its computing budget. The limitation is the measured prior
dependence, about 10–13% per coarse joint cell, which is comparable to the generator differences a
user would need to resolve. It motivates the calibrated joint tests of §11, a separate inference that
reports no measured cross section.
Variability and interval behavior at five iterations. Figure 27 compares ensemble residual
means and repeat standard deviations at the same setting. At historical W3 truth, EW29 has mean
residual 23.67 % and repeat SD 0.56 %; EW7 has −6.81 % and 0.30 %. Small spread does not imply
small bias. Coarser cells can have smaller residuals and spread, but they estimate different integrals,
so their smaller residuals and spread are not an improvement in precision on an unchanged quantity.
No matching repeat ensembles exist at more iterations or larger OmniFold capacity, so no tradeoff
can be inferred.
The statistical intervals of the development assessment take their width from 100 bootstrap
replicas of one nominal development experiment and do not recompute it for each assessment
experiment (Fig. 28). At nominal truth, EW41 contains the truth in 15 of 40 68% intervals (37.5%,
pointwise exact 95% interval [22.7%, 54.2%]), so a pooled pass cannot qualify this cell. A 16063

Figure 27: Estimator R at five iterations. Each supported J or H2 cell gives the mean and SD of
(fˆ − ftrue )/ftrue in the same background-inclusive ensemble: 40 nominal experiments, 20 each for W2

Truth panels and reporting cells form no frontier across estimator settings.
experiment check with the width averaged over six bootstrap estimates changes the pull scales but
does not validate the real-data procedure, which uses the data’s own width. Finite replica noise
explains much of the apparent variation among the six estimates.
A paired perturbation study supports the containment of rounding variability in the refit-perreplica bootstrap of R. Twenty edge-safe data jitters (uniform input perturbations within half a
single-precision unit in the last place that keep the fine-grid membership fixed) give a J-cell median
SD of 0.228 %, against a bootstrap SD of 0.368 %. Fifty bootstrap replicas with jittered partners
give a median consistency ratio of 1.02 and an estimated numerical share of 0.37 of the bootstrap
variance. In this design, adding the numerical variance to the bootstrap variance would count it
twice. The stability failure and the study’s scope are in §9.3, interval coverage in §9.5.
Prior sensitivity is not a measured real-data bias. Real-data prior shifts correlate with
minus the noise-free residual at the same historical truth (Fig. 29). For W3 the correlation over J
cells is 0.92, yet |shift| ≥ |residual| holds in only 71% of supported J cells (52% of H2 cells, 54% of
EW cells). Maximum shifts over five historical vertices have medians 12.0%, 9.6% and 13.4% on
J, H2 and EW; excluding D1 gives 11.2%, 4.3% and 9.9%. These envelopes are sensitivities: they
provide no probabilities, covariance components or guaranteed bias bounds, and the truth of the
data has not been shown to lie within their span.
Deciding the tradeoff would require sharing pseudo-data and MC splits across a small set of
iteration and capacity settings and measuring bias, variance and mean squared error for common
functionals. That comparison would not validate intervals, which also requires an explicit treatment
of physical nuisances and model dependence. No new ensemble enters these studies.

estimator variant with nominal-truth bias correction (1,200 pseudo-experiments over three truths).

Figure 29: Historical W3 at five iterations. Horizontal gives minus the noise-free relative truth
residual; vertical gives the relative shift in real-data R when its MC prior changes to W3, normalized

reduction of the signal rate with a weak Eavail slope (below). No frequentist coverage is therefore
claimed for any five-dimensional interval. The declared coverage test of the sixteen-cell result did
not run to a result: its first stage stopped under a fail-closed rule declared in advance (§10.5).
Figure 36 shows the coverage of each reported integral under the rate reduction. The intervals
divide each estimate by 1 + β, where β is the relative closure bias at nominal truth, and add
the standard error of β times the corrected estimate in quadrature to the statistical standard
deviation. A quantity fails when the upper confidence bound on its coverage lies below 0.66 for
68% intervals or below 0.94 for 95% intervals. The second truth (the Eavail tilt) multiplies the true
signal by 1 + 0.2 (Eavail /100 GeV − 12 ) without renormalization, with the background unchanged;
below Eavail = 3 GeV the weight is 0.900–0.906. Under that change, 51 of the 153 reported cell and
projection integrals have 68% coverage confidently below nominal, with coverage as low as 0.060,
and 42 of the 153 fail at 95%. At nominal truth two of the 153 fail at 95%, the lowest coverage being
0.850.
Why the intervals miscover under the rate reduction. The diagnosis below uses the stored
outputs and the code. The rate change itself is recovered: in the one locally stored pseudo-experiment
the total changes by −9.58 % against −9.56 % injected, and over the 400 the total covers at 0.74
(68%), so the miscoverage is not a normalization failure. Nor are the intervals too narrow: they
use one nominal bootstrap σ at every truth, which for 10% fewer events is slightly too wide apart
from the background not being redrawn (inferred spread about 1.15σ at W ≥ 3 GeV), and setting
the spread equal to the half-width leaves the failing quantities’ coverage at 0.06–0.22. The purity
subtraction does not apply a nominal signal fraction to the reduced data: it removes the absolute
nominal background expectation, which is unchanged, and in the stored pseudo-experiment the
subtracted total agrees with the pseudo-signal to 60 of 3.2 million events. The miscoverage is a
residual bias that the nominal-truth bias correction does not remove. The rate change adds a positive
bias that grows with W , about 0.2 % for 1.4 ≤ W < 2.2 GeV, 0.4 % for 2.2 ≤ W < 3 GeV, and
0.7–1.1 % for W ≥ 3 GeV, which is 1.3–2.6 standard deviations against intervals of 0.16–0.43%,
whatever the sign of the nominal bias β; removing that bias restores coverage to 0.62–0.82 at 68%
and 0.92–0.99 at 95%. These per-quantity biases and spreads are inferred, not measured: the stored
output records only hit counts, so each quantity’s mean shift and spread come from a Gaussian fit
to its 68% and 95% coverage, checked against one recorded value (EW17: +1.04% fitted against
+1.05%). The source of the W -graded term is not established: its size and ordering roughly match,
under an approximate assignment of background fractions to W bins, the rise of the background
fraction by 0.111 fbkg , but the term may also arise from the estimator alone. A rerun of five of the
same pseudo-experiments, without background and with the background scaled by 0.9 so that the
background-to-signal ratio is restored, did not decide between the two: restoring the ratio raised
the residual for W ≥ 3 GeV rather than removing it, and without background the residual persists.
Agreement in the marginal normalizations thus coexists with a substantial residual bias under the
tested deformation, and whether that bias has the same origin as the regularization bias of §9.3 is
not established.

9.6

The five-axis covariance of the configuration-(a) central value and its
(Eavail , W ) projection

Status. The five-axis covariance used in this note is the variant centered on the central value (cv);
its (Eavail , W ) projection has 42 cells. The file and the projection are identified by their SHA-256
checksums (Ref. [37], identities already fixed). The covariance does not meet the acceptance criteria
of its specification, and its metadata mark it as not passing and as not to be used. The specification

requires the null bound to be fixed before production, which was not done for this covariance, so
it cannot meet that requirement. This note nevertheless uses this covariance and its projection.
The use does not extend to any other covariance built to the same specification, including the two
companion covariances of the measurements below.
Construction. The covariance projects the corrected five-axis statistical covariance as M C5D M ⊤ ,
without rebuilding it in the destination bins, and uses corrected systematic endpoints. Re-selected
event loops bound the selection migrations that the single-pass shifted observables omit (§4.7). On
the 10 694 reported cells of (pT , p∥ , Eavail , q3 , W ), the covariance is

Here vblk = diag
V Cb is the vertical block-sum diagonal; vuni is the unified-throw covariance
diagonal centered on the central value. Where the denominator is zero, g = 1 is set by definition, not
computed. The band groups V (vertical), R (residual) and A (re-selected lateral) must be disjoint
and exhaust the 45 bands. The same inflation of the vertical bands can be transferred onto a sweep
block, Csyst → (Csyst − Cvert ) + G Cvert G (G = diag g), only if the low-rank treatment, the mean
shift and the selection-support bound above are documented (a condition set in July 2026). This
transfer is positive-semidefinite by construction and never falls below the block baseline, whereas a
direct rank-N swap is not positive-semidefinite.
Centering, explicitly. Per-band blocks Cb use the MAT-conformant mean-centered ±1σ estimator
cv . The unified-throw centerings differ by the
of §A.2.5. Inflation in the CV-centered variant uses vuni
